- **Phase 07.2 (`07.2-header-mutation-filter`) — DONE as of this commit.** The first concrete pluggable HTTP filter — `envoy.filters.http.header_mutation` — landed end-to-end through the 07.1-established framework: `envoy-config` HeaderMutation schema (`HttpFilterTypedConfig::HeaderMutation` variant + 5 supporting structs + `AppendAction` enum; all `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`) + validator extension (`validate_header_mutation_entries` + `is_valid_rfc7230_token`; 3 new `ConfigError` variants — `EmptyHeaderMutationKey` / `InvalidHeaderMutationKey` / `UnsupportedHeaderMutationAppendAction`) + `crates/envoy-filter/src/header_mutation.rs` (`HeaderMutationFilter` runtime + `RuntimeHeaderMutation` + `build_from_config` + `apply_mutations` append/overwrite semantics; build-time key lowercasing + config→runtime `AppendAction` lowering) + the `test-util` Cargo feature on `envoy-filter` (the doctrinally-correct cross-crate test-stub mechanism — a feature flag, not a new top-level dep) + 9 H1/H2 HCM filter-chain integration tests + fuzz corpus seed `crates/envoy-config/fuzz/corpus/parse_bootstrap/hcm_header_mutation_filter.yaml` + the new differential fixture `tests/fixtures/0013-http-filter-header-mutation/` (HeaderMutation in front of Router on a Router-proxied route to an `Http1EchoBackend`; bilaterally asserts the decode-side `x-filter-stamp: phase-07` echoed back through the backend body AND the encode-side `x-filter-response-stamp: phase-07` on the response headers) + in-process backstop `crates/envoy-bin/tests/http_filter_header_mutation.rs`. The `envoy-accesslog` foundation crate (sole-dep-owner of any access-log surface — module decomposition: `record` + `file_sink` + `default_format` + `error` + a doc-comment-only `sink` placeholder; ships with `tokio` + `bytes` + `tracing` + `thiserror` only; no `time` / `chrono` / `async_trait` / `regex`) + Envoy default-format emitter (hand-rolled atop `std::time::SystemTime` with a Gregorian calendar arithmetic helper covering full leap-year semantics; ISO-8601 `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ` byte-strict 24-byte rendering) + concrete `FileSink` (hand-rolled atop `tokio::fs::OpenOptions::append(true)` + `Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<File>>` for serializing concurrent emissions; the `Sink` trait deferred per parent-06 SPEC §3 D8.2 option (c) so HCMConfig.access_log carries `Vec<Arc<envoy_accesslog::FileSink>>` concretely — avoids the `async_trait = "0.1"` foundations grant) + HCM access-log wiring on both H1 and H2 codepaths (factored single-dispatch site after each writer arm populates the captured `response_status_for_log` / `response_body_len` / `response_headers_for_log` / `upstream_host_for_log` locals; emission fire-and-forget with `tracing::warn!` on per-sink failure per architectural Rule 4) + envoy-config schema additions (`HttpConnectionManagerConfig.access_log: Vec<AccessLogConfig>` with serde-tagged `AccessLogTypedConfig` enum gating on `name = "envoy.access_loggers.file"` + `@type = .../FileAccessLog`; new `ConfigError::UnsupportedAccessLogType` + `InvalidAccessLogPath` variants; format-string customization OUT of scope) + differential harness extension (`Driver::Http1WithAccessLog` + per-token `AccessLogLineRule` enum + hand-rolled `tokenize_default_format` state-machine tokenizer + `assert_access_log_lines_equivalent` per-token cascade) + first-time population of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Access log field mapping` section (14 rows). **Historical reference (preserved per D-3.4 + D-3.5 — the phase-10 state-6 close-out commit `e24053e`'s `## Next expected skill` block describing the post-phase-10 feature-family brainstorm session; preserved verbatim below; "the next session" + "THIS commit" references resolve to the phase-11 state-1 brainstorm session [THIS commit] + `e24053e` in their original context):**

Per `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §9 (Feature Families — phases 09 and onward) + `SKILL_ROUTING.md` (state 0 — phase not yet in ROADMAP.md → `superpowers:brainstorming`): the next session — operating as the **post-phase-10 feature-family brainstorm session** — invokes **`superpowers:brainstorming`** scoped to the next feature-family phase. **Feature-family ordering is a brainstorm-time decision; no family is pre-assigned.** The 11 feature-family headings seeded in ROADMAP.md (`BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §9):

- **HTTP filters family** — header manipulation, cors, compression, fault, local+global rate limit, jwt_authn, rbac, ext_authz, ext_proc, oauth2, csrf, buffer, lua, wasm, adaptive concurrency, admission control, bandwidth limit. **First two concrete rows (phase 09 `local_ratelimit` + phase 10 `rbac`) are `done`.**
- **Network filters family** — redis, mongo, kafka_broker, thrift, zookeeper [scope TBD], echo, direct_response, sni_cluster, rbac network.
- **Load balancing family** — least_request, random, ring_hash, maglev, subset LB, locality-weighted LB, priority load balancing, panic thresholds.
- **Upstream robustness family** — active health checks (HTTP/TCP/gRPC/custom), outlier detection variants, circuit breakers, retries + hedging, per-protocol connection pooling.
- **HTTP/3 + QUIC family** — quinn transport, downstream H3 listener, upstream H3 cluster, h3spec gate.
- **gRPC family** — gRPC bridge, gRPC-Web, gRPC-JSON transcoding, interop conformance.
- **xDS / dynamic config family** — ADS, delta xDS, LDS, CDS, RDS, EDS, SDS, RTDS, reconnection, initial-fetch timeout.
- **Observability family** — gRPC ALS, OTLP access log, OTel/Zipkin/Jaeger/Datadog/XRay tracing, stats sinks, tap filter.
- **Runtime + hot restart family.**
- **WASM host family** — own multi-phase sub-project; ABI, engine binding, proxy-wasm conformance.
- **Deprecated / edge features** — explicit out-of-scope ADRs unless later re-opened.

The brainstorm session's concrete acts (mirrors the phase-09 + phase-10 state-1 brainstorm cadence — phase-10 state-1 brainstorm commit `c73f44f` is the freshest precedent): brainstorm the family-pick + first-feature-pick along the documented dimensions (carryforward closure value / foundation pressure / architectural risk / contract-surface maturity / first-phase scope tractability), pick the next feature-family phase + its slug, add the row to `docs/envoy-rust/ROADMAP.md` (the family's first or next concrete row beneath its already-seeded heading) with `status: planned`, create `docs/envoy-rust/phases/NN-<slug>/`, land the phase `SPEC.md`, and end (per `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §5.1 one-state-per-session). The session AFTER the state-1 brainstorm writes `PLAN.md` per `superpowers:writing-plans` (state 2).

### ADR numbering after the phase-03 split

The parent-phase-03 SPEC (`03-tls-tcp/SPEC.md`, committed at SHA `a3f3474`) projected three phase-03 ADRs numbered 0017 (`rcgen` + `tempfile`), 0018 (`tokio-rustls` + `rustls-pemfile`), 0019 (split phase 03). The ADR-0017 split decision (landed at `f256d2c`) took the actual next-sequential number at split time, so each projected ADR shifted in-tree:

- **ADR-0017** — split phase 03 into 03.1 + 03.2 (landed at `f256d2c`; was parent-SPEC §7's projected ADR-0019).
- **ADR-0018** — `rcgen` + `tempfile` permitted as dev-test-harness-only foundations (landed at `f93a062` during 03.1 Task 1; was parent-SPEC §7's projected ADR-0017).
- **ADR-0019** — `tokio-rustls` + `rustls-pemfile` covered by the rustls foundations grant (landed at `f93a062` during 03.1 Task 1; was parent-SPEC §7's projected ADR-0018).

The sub-phase SPECs (03.1 + 03.2) cite ADR-0017 for the renumbering and rewrite each expected ADR with its actual landed number. The parent SPEC (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/03-tls-tcp/SPEC.md`) is preserved unedited per D-3.4 / D-3.5.

The parent-phase-02 SPEC (`02-tcp-proxy/SPEC.md`, committed at SHA `50349da`) projected three phase-02 ADRs numbered 0013 (typed_config), 0014 (host-docker + host-gateway), 0015 (enable_half_close false default). The ADR-0013 split decision (landed at `1c38ca9`) took the actual next-sequential number at split time, so each projected ADR shifted by +1 in-tree:

- **ADR-0014** — YAML-native `typed_config` deserialization (landed at `6d1f8d6` during 02.1 Task 1; was parent-SPEC §7's ADR-0013).

- **ADR-0016** — phase 02 TCP proxy runs with Envoy's default `enable_half_close: false` (landed at `435c6fa` during 02.2 Task 1; was parent-SPEC §7's ADR-0015).

### Phase-04.3 rollovers (from REVIEW.md §3–§4)

The 04.3 REVIEW landed with zero Critical items, zero Important items in the 04.3 surface itself, 1 Important cross-phase carryforward (C-1), and 4 awareness-only Minor findings. Eight in-phase review-fix commits closed substantive findings before propagation (Tasks 2/8/9/11/12/13/14/16). Items closed in 04.3:

- **04.1 M3** (`envoy-http1`'s forward-looking `envoy-cluster` path-dep): structurally closed via `HCMConfig.cluster_mgr: Arc<envoy_cluster::ClusterManager>` at Task 9 commit `3fdf960`. (Note: PROGRESS Task 16 attribution to Task 5 is imprecise — see M3-correction below; closure verdict is accurate.)
- **04.1 M6** (`drive_http1` per-function unit test): "practically closed" — Task 6/9/13 added end-to-end exercise; the strict per-function in-isolation unit test was never added. Hedged closure documented.
- **04.2 M10** (PLAN.md late-landing cadence): closed cleanly. The 04.3 planner committed PLAN.md as standalone pre-Task-1 commit `c02eea7` (2026-04-27 16:43) before any task commits, breaking the 04.1 → 04.2 inline-PLAN precedent.
- **#12 / phase-02.1 M1 (`Cluster::name()` accessor, multi-phase carryforward)**: closed at Task 9 commit `3fdf960`. `pub fn Cluster::name(&self) -> &str` lands at `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs:24-26`; `pub fn ClusterHandle::name(&self) -> &str` at `:60-62`; field-level `#[allow(dead_code)]` removed; consumed by router-arm `tracing::warn!` log lines at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:208`/`:248`/`:265`. The carryforward chain phase-02.1 → 02.2 → 03.1 → 03.2 → 04.1 → 04.2 → 04.3 ends here.

Items carrying forward to phase 05+ / hardening / fixture-hardening sub-phase:

**C-1 (Important, cross-phase systemic regression).** Docker-gated `host.docker.internal`/`STATIC` regression on fixtures 0003/0004/0005/0006/0008. Originates at phase-02.2's ADR-0015 landing (where `host.docker.internal` was introduced as the BACKEND_HOST substitution for cross-container reachability via `host-gateway`); subsequent phases 02.2 / 03.1 / 03.2 / 04.1 / 04.2 did not push to CI between phase-02.1 close (run `24913934580`) and 04.3 task 14 (run `25106213773`), so the regression has been latent across **five phases**. Envoy v1.33's tightened `socket_address.address` parse semantics expect either a literal IP (under `STATIC`) or DNS resolution opt-in (under `STRICT_DNS` / `LOGICAL_DNS`):

```
[critical][main] [source/server/server.cc:416] error initializing config '/etc/envoy/envoy.yaml':
malformed IP address: host.docker.internal. Consider setting resolver_name or setting cluster type
to 'STRICT_DNS' or 'LOGICAL_DNS'
```

The 04.3 REVIEW reviewer's verdict choice was **(b) "Approved with M-track follow-ups" — defer to a dedicated post-04.3 fixture-hardening sub-phase** (or roll into phase 05 brainstorm scope), on the rationale of cross-phase scope spanning 5 fixtures and 4 phases, schema growth not in 04.3's planned deliverable set, original-budget-fit concerns, and the fact that fixture 0008 inherits the broken pattern uniformly with the four pre-04.3 fixtures. The recommended forward work:

1. Add `ClusterType::StrictDns` to the envoy-config schema at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::ClusterType` (currently single-variant `Static` enum at lines 60-62); this also closes the dormant phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 (positive `ClusterType::Static` variant-name regression guard) in the same scope by giving the second variant.
2. Validator accept path for `STRICT_DNS` cluster type.
3. Coordinated-edit of the 5 affected fixtures (`tests/fixtures/{0003,0004,0005,0006,0008}/envoy.yaml` + per-fixture `envoy-rust.yaml` mirror) to use `type: STRICT_DNS` where `host.docker.internal` is the backend host literal.
4. Re-push to CI to confirm green Docker-gated runs across all 5 fixtures including 0008.

The phase-05 brainstorm session is the natural place to choose between (a) folding C-1 into phase 05's scope as a Task-1 preamble, (b) splitting C-1 into a separate fixture-hardening sub-phase (e.g. `04.4-fixture-hardening` or `05.0-fixture-hardening`) that lands before phase-05 HTTP/2 work begins, or (c) ratifying the deferral and continuing against the unit-test gate. Doctrinally option (b) is the cleanest shape per the M-track follow-up posture.

**M3-correction** (awareness-only). PROGRESS Task 16 says M3 closed at Task 5; the actual structural consumption of `envoy-cluster` from `envoy-http1` lands at Task 9 (commit `3fdf960`) via `HCMConfig.cluster_mgr`, not Task 5 (which only confirmed the path-dep is active in the build graph). Verdict (M3 closed) is accurate; the proximate-cause attribution is imprecise. **Track forward only if a future audit cites M3's closure commit** — leave PROGRESS-on-disk as-is.

**M-claim** (awareness-only, 04.1 M6 carryforward). Strict per-function `drive_http1` unit test never landed; first end-to-end exercise via fixture 0008 is currently masked by C-1's Docker-gated regression. **Track forward to whichever phase first adds a third Driver::Http1 consumer OR the C-1 fixture-hardening sub-phase** (which would unblock the end-to-end exercise via fixture 0008 once the cluster-type fix lands).

**M-payload** (awareness-only). `payload.bin` for fixture 0008 is empty (0 bytes), not the literal request bytes the 04.3 SPEC §3 D4 worked example shows. `Driver::Http1` constructs the request from driver fields, not by reading `payload.bin`. **Track forward only if a future phase adds a `Driver::Http1Raw`** (or similar) that reads `payload.bin` directly; if so, fixture 0008 may need to be amended to populate the file.

**M-spec-equiv** (awareness-only). Fixture 0008's `expectations.yaml` carries the working shape (per-driver `expected_headers` + 2 `equivalence` keys) rather than the SPEC §3 D4 worked example's 3-key `equivalence` shape. SPEC drift was anticipated and well-disclosed in PROGRESS Task 15. SPEC §3 D4 is now slightly stale on this point but is closed at this commit; **no follow-up needed**.

**M-payload-divergence** (awareness-only). `request_headers_to_remove` + `generate_request_id: false` on Envoy side only is intentional and load-bearing — neutralizes Envoy v1.33's default 6-header injection (x-forwarded-for, x-forwarded-proto, x-request-id, x-envoy-expected-rq-timeout-ms, x-envoy-internal, x-envoy-external-address) that would otherwise land in the deterministic-echo body and break byte-equivalence. The right long-term resolution is to extend envoy-rust to emit the same headers per parent SPEC §4's "default plan (b)" / "follow-on (a)" decision tree. **Track forward to a future phase that adds these headers to envoy-rust's HCM emission set** — natural fit at the access-log family (phase 06+) or whichever phase first needs request-side header injection for production realism.

**M-architectural-claim** (awareness-only, pre-existing carve-out). `httparse` lives at three Cargo.toml entries (`envoy-http1`, `envoy-bin`'s admin endpoint, differential harness). 04.3's new `httparse::Response::parse` use site is correctly inside `envoy-http1`. The pre-existing carve-outs at envoy-bin's admin endpoint and differential harness are tracked from 04.1 forward; eventual consolidation is well outside 04.3's scope. **Track forward to whichever phase next touches admin or routes the differential harness's response parser through `envoy-http1::Client::send_request`.**

### Phase-05.1 rollovers (from REVIEW.md §3–§4)

### Phase-05.2 rollovers (from REVIEW.md §3–§5)

The 05.2 REVIEW landed with zero Critical, **3 Important findings (I1 / I2 / I3 — all scoped, mechanical, hardening-or-ergonomic in character; none are correctness bugs)**, and 12 awareness-only Minor findings (M1 through M12). The reviewer recommended the cross-phase carryforward path for all 3 Important findings (parallel to phase-04.3's C-1 disposition) rather than re-entering state 3. Eight in-phase review-fix commits closed substantive findings before propagation across Tasks 4-13 (`703febf` task 4 / `2a9fe96` task 5 / `ba93ab7` task 6 / `cb11faa` task 7 / `c35f0b0` task 8 / `a0b7e67` task 9 / `f6ee022` task 10 / `3f117a3` task 11 / `f6a0ad4` task 13); the post-CI parser-rewrite at `dac3f8b` reshaped the h2spec output parser to match the actual h2spec output shape (section-context tracking + canonical summary line + 2 unit tests locking the parser shape).

Items closed in 05.2:

- **Conditional ADR-0024 / parent-05 SPEC §7 / 05.2 SPEC §1 layer 2 `http` typed-surface scoping**: **RESOLVED** as ADR-0027 with appropriate provenance per the established phase-03 / phase-05.4 renumbering precedent. Landed at Task 1 commit `9c2b2fd` inline with the `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml` scaffold creation. The renumbering provenance: projected as conditional ADR-0024 in 05.2 SPEC §7 + parent-05 SPEC §7; renumbered to ADR-0027 at landing time because phase 05.4 landed ADR-0024 / ADR-0025 / ADR-0026 between SPEC writeup (parent-05 state-2 commit `f1804a7`) and 05.2 execution. Mirrors the phase-03 ADR-renumbering precedent. The renumbering chain phase-03 → phase-05 SPEC-projection → landing-time discipline is preserved.
- **Conditional ADR-0028 (h2spec gate-mechanics decision)**: **RESOLVED — explicitly NOT landed.** Rationale recorded inline in PROGRESS Task 13: the gate-mechanics (≥95% pass + every failing test enumerated in `known-failures.txt` + every entry in `known-failures.txt` actually fails) are mechanically deterministic and self-documenting at the runner code level. No decision worth ADR-shaped permanent record. Setting this precedent is itself a doctrine signal: not every conditional-projected ADR has to land.
- **Cross-phase C-1 carryforward** (substantively closed at 05.4 Task 7 verification commit `a8c2364`): 05.2 inherits the GREEN baseline cleanly via CI run `25294149612` — all 9 Docker-gated fixtures (0001-0008 inherited + new 0009) green simultaneously.
- **The `BuildOutcome::Proxy` codepath compiling-but-stubbed posture**: per SPEC §4 "not deferred — confirmed in scope" item 4: the 502-stub at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:117-134` correctly typechecks against the route-walk's `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arm and the stream task drops cleanly. 05.3 will replace this stub with the H1-or-H2 dispatch.

Items carrying forward to phase 05.3 (recommended closure-targets):

- **I1 (Important; CI h2spec tarball lacks integrity verification).** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:43-49` provisions h2spec via `curl -fsSL ... | sudo tar xz -C /usr/local/bin` with no SHA-256, no GPG, no tag-pinning beyond version string. Blast radius is contained (CI runner ephemeral; envoy-rust source checked out before install) but a release-pipeline takeover or DNS hijack could inject arbitrary code. **Fix sketch**: capture published SHA-256 from the GitHub release page, hardcode, verify after download. **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3 (or as a standalone 05.x security-pass).**
- **I2 (Important; `Http2Error` variant overload at the response-emission edge).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/response.rs:60-69` openly admits two misnomers: `send_response()` failures map to `H2StreamAccept` (line 77; "accept" implies inbound), `send_data()` failures map to `H2BodyRead` (line 81; "read" implies inbound). The variant names imply inbound/accept paths but are used for outbound/send paths. Source `h2::Error` retains diagnostic fidelity, but the typed wrapper is misleading on stack traces and log lines. **Fix sketch**: add `H2ResponseSend { source: h2::Error }` + `H2BodyWrite { source: h2::Error }` variants (or rename `H2StreamAccept` → `H2StreamIo` if a single duplex variant is preferred). Public API change but no external consumers yet — additive variants are cheap. **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3, BEFORE the upstream H2 client introduces additional H2 error sites that would amplify the misnaming.**
- **I3 (Important; `MalformedH2HeaderBlock` is a coarse catch-all hiding three distinct failure modes).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/error.rs:40-50` documents the variant as covering structural pseudo-header issues, non-token byte names, and non-UTF-8 byte values; in practice it's also raised for `HeaderName::from_bytes` failure on the response side, `HeaderValue::from_str` failure, and `HttpResponse::builder().body(())` failure (`crates/envoy-http2/src/response.rs:49-57`). When a request-side translation failure hits this variant, an operator looking at logs cannot tell whether it was a non-UTF-8 header-value byte (request-side, defensible 400), an unrepresentable response status, or a stale state-machine bug elsewhere. **Fix sketch**: split into `InvalidHeaderName { name: String }`, `InvalidHeaderValue { name: String }`, `InvalidResponseHead`. Public API impact bounded since callers currently only `match` exhaustively in tests. **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3.**
- **M2 (per-stream `tokio::spawn` task carries no per-test or per-fixture timeout).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:78-82` spawns each H2 stream task without a wall-clock budget. Latent in 05.2 because the only `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arm is the 502-stub which returns synchronously. **Fix sketch**: add a per-stream `tokio::time::timeout` budget when 05.3 wires the upstream H2 dispatch (the budget fits naturally there since the upstream call is the long-tail). **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3 or 08.**
- **M8 (502 Bad Gateway stub body literal mentions phase 05.3).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:132` emits body `b"upstream H2 not yet wired (sub-phase 05.3)\n"`. SPEC §6 local signpost 21 explicitly accepts "doctrine-line is sufficient." When 05.3 replaces the stub, this literal disappears. **Disposition: trivial 05.3 entry-point check; closes at 05.3.**
- **M10 (`differential::Driver::Http2` variant lacks `extra_headers`).** `tests/differential/src/lib.rs:86-96` carries `method/path/host/expected_status/expected_body/expected_headers` but no `extra_headers: Vec<(String, String)>` field (compare `Http1Probe` at lines 175-191). 05.2's only fixture (0009) does not need it; 05.3 may. The `drive_http2` helper already accepts an `extra_headers` parameter (`tests/differential/src/lib.rs:806`); only the enum variant lacks the field. **Fix sketch**: at 05.3 when fixture 0010 lands, add the field with `#[serde(default)]` and thread it through the dispatch arm. **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3.**
- **M11 (`Http2Error::MissingAuthority` recovery path is RFC-soft).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:78-82` (the spawn site) logs the per-stream error via `tracing::error!` and lets the task end without sending any response. RFC 9113 §8.3.1 requires a request without `:authority` (and without a `Host:` header) to either be rejected by the codec layer or generate a 400 from the server. envoy-rust currently produces no response. **Fix sketch**: raise a 400 Bad Request from the stream task on `MissingAuthority` (and other request-translation failures) instead of dropping silently. Maps to envoy-rust's H1 `synth_400` posture. **Disposition: carry forward to 05.3 or 06+.**

Awareness-only Minor findings (no scheduled close):

- **M1** — `error::Http2Error::BadStatusCode` is essentially unreachable in production (defense-in-depth at `response.rs:38-40`; `from_u16` only fails for values < 100 or ≥ 1000 or = 0; route-walk and validator already constrain to `100..=599`). Awareness only — defense-in-depth is the appropriate posture.
- **M3** — `h2_protocol_options_max_concurrent_streams_applied` test is `#[ignore]`-d at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:515-527` because h2 0.4's public API doesn't deterministically surface peer SETTINGS observability without racing the response loop. Wire-effect test is genuinely blocked by upstream library shape. Partial coverage shifts to `codec.rs::tests::build_h2_server_applies_protocol_options` (which only verifies the setter is callable). **Track until upstream `h2` crate exposes deterministic SETTINGS observability** (or until envoy-rust lands its own h2-server harness with internal observability).
- **M4** — `expectations.yaml` syntax convention is undocumented. SPEC §3 D6 documents `expected_headers: { rule: set_equal_modulo_allow_list }` (struct shape), but the fixture ships `expected_headers: set_equal_modulo_allow_list` (string-shaped — matches the unit-variant `Http1HeaderRule::SetEqualModuloAllowList` deserializer). Behavior correct; SPEC text stale. **Close at next SPEC editing pass alongside M9.**
- **M5** — `h2spec` runner relies on `target/<profile>/envoy-bin` convention with a fragile `.parent().parent().parent()` walk at `tests/conformance/h2spec/tests/h2spec_runner.rs:177-204`. Cross-crate-stable but path-position-fragile. **Fix sketch**: factor `locate_envoy_bin` into a small `envoy-test-support` workspace member; defer until N=3 consumers (currently 2: differential + h2spec).
- **M6** — h2spec parser does not surface `skipped` count in the gate diagnostic. Pass-rate gate is `passed / (passed + failed)` (correct), but if skipped count drifts (e.g., a future h2spec release marks half its corpus as skipped), the operator would not see this in the gate output. **Trivial; close opportunistically in 05.3.**
- **M7** — The `envoy-http2` crate's `[dev-dependencies]` lists `envoy-cluster` for `ClusterManager::empty()` in HCM tests. The `empty()` constructor is `#[doc(hidden)] pub` per `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs:81-99`; the doc-comment explicitly addresses cross-crate test consumption. Cross-crate use of a `#[doc(hidden)]` constructor is a soft architectural smell. **Defer; current shape is not actively harmful.**
- **M9** — h2spec config carries no `node` field at the runner level but the YAML uses `node:`. envoy-rust treats `node` as optional + open-shaped, so this works. SPEC §3 D7 example for the runner config did not include a `node` block. **Close at next SPEC editing pass alongside M4.**
- **M12** — The `h2_handshake_fails_on_garbage_preamble` test is permissive on the close shape. Accepts `Ok(0)` / `Ok(non-zero)` provided bytes are not an HTTP/1.1 status line / `Err(_)` (RST/ECONNRESET). Catches anything-but-success but loses signal. **Fix sketch**: tighten to a positive assertion (deadline of 1 second AND received bytes do not include `:status: 200` or any known-good H2 frame). **Trivial; close opportunistically.**

- **Phase-04.1 REVIEW M5/M9** (Cargo.lock cadence): 05.2 continues the inline-at-scaffold cadence per parent-05 SPEC §6 signpost 14. No policy change; M5/M9 carry forward to whichever phase ratifies a single cadence.
- **Phase-02.2 REVIEW M1** (SIGKILL-on-Drop posture for envoy-bin subprocess): `crates/envoy-bin/tests/http2_direct_response.rs` mirrors the existing posture (`kill_on_drop(true)` + bare `drop(child)`); 05.2 does not parallelize `run_fixture` so M1 continues unchanged per SPEC §6 local signpost 22.
- **Phase-04.1 REVIEW M7** (`TlsAcceptingHandler.inner` concrete-typed): re-deferred per parent-05 SPEC §4 to whichever phase ships ALPN-driven dispatch. 05.2 does not generalize; M7 continues unchanged. The H2+TLS rejection (`Http2OverTlsNotSupported` + the runtime symmetric bail at `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs:269-277`) is the explicit gate.

The 502-stub was replaced with the symmetric H1-or-H2 router-arm dispatch on `cluster.upstream_protocol()`; the literal disappeared. Items unblocked but not closed in 05.3:

- **05.2 REVIEW M2** (per-stream `tokio::spawn` task carries no per-test or per-fixture timeout): **NOT CLOSED.** Per 05.3 PROGRESS preamble paragraph 3, the SPEC §3 D4 dispatch path does not edit per-stream task timeouts; M2 carries forward awareness-only. The natural close site shifts to a phase 06+ hardening pass or whichever phase first surfaces an upstream H2 stall in production-shaped traffic.

Items carrying forward to phase 06+ (recommended closure-targets):

- **I1 (Important; ADR-0028's option-(B) deferral leaves an H1-listener × H2-cluster silent runtime protocol-misnegotiation).** ADR-0028 deliberately defers the H1-listener H2-arm dispatch (the H1 listener cannot dispatch to a cluster whose `upstream_protocol = Http2` because adding `envoy-http2` as a dep on `envoy-http1` would cycle). The deferral is correct doctrine — but the deferred path is not gated at parse time, so a configuration with `codec_type: AUTO` (or `HTTP1`) on the listener and a cluster with `typed_extension_protocol_options.HttpProtocolOptions.http2_protocol_options` set does NOT fail validation; it silently runs H1-on-the-wire to an H2-only upstream backend, which would either get rejected by the backend or produce a confusing 502. **Fix sketch**: add `ConfigError::Http2ClusterFromHttp1Listener { listener: String, cluster: String }` parse-time gate at the envoy-config validator (cross-validate listener `codec_type` against each cluster's `upstream_protocol` per route), or add a runtime defense at the H1 router-arm `BuildOutcome::Proxy` site that catches the misconfiguration with an explicit log + 502. **Disposition: carry forward to phase 06+.** Phase 06's brainstorm should consider folding I1 as a Task-1 preamble (the parse-time gate is a small validator extension).
- **I2 (Important; typed-error chain dissolved at the dispatch site).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs`'s symmetric H1-or-H2 router-arm dispatch (Task 7, `52b3635`) collapses `Http2Error` variants to a 502 string at the wire via `to_string()`-shaping. The reviewer's specific finding: when the upstream H2 client returns `Http2Error::UpstreamConnect` (TCP refused) vs `H2ClientHandshake` (h2 handshake failed) vs `H2RecvBody` (body drain mid-stream), the on-the-wire response is identical (`502 Bad Gateway` with body `upstream connect error`-ish text); operators looking at logs see the typed variant in `tracing::error!`, but the response gives no signal. **Fix sketch**: structured `x-envoy-upstream-rq-failure-reason` response header reflecting the typed variant (envoy parity), or per-variant body text. Public API change but additive. **Disposition: carry forward to phase 06+.**
- **05.2 REVIEW I1+I2+I3** (CI tarball SHA-256 / Http2Error write-path variant rename / MalformedH2HeaderBlock overload split): **NOT CLOSED in 05.3.** Per 05.3 PROGRESS preamble paragraph 3, the SPEC §3 D1 explicitly preserved the 05.2 codec-side variants unchanged ("the 05.2 codec-side variants ... stay unchanged"). All three carryforwards continue to phase 06+.
- **05.2 REVIEW M11** (RFC-soft `MissingAuthority` recovery path): **NOT CLOSED in 05.3.** The per-stream task error handling at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs` was unedited at the upstream-direction work; M11 continues to carry forward awareness-only.

Awareness-only Minor findings (per REVIEW §3 M1-M8):

- **M1** — `crates/envoy-http2/src/client.rs::ClientStream::send_request`'s comment-vs-code drift: the doc-comment claims "synthesizes :method/:path/:authority/:scheme: http"; the implementation also synthesizes `:scheme: https` if the connection is TLS-bearing (which 05.3 doesn't yet exercise but the code branches on it). Awareness only — defensive future-proofing. **Track until phase 06+ TLS+H2 work surfaces this.**
- **M2** — `Client::connect`'s `tokio::spawn` to drive the h2 connection has no `JoinHandle` retention; the spawned task is fire-and-forget. If the connection task panics (h2 internal bug), the panic is logged via tracing but not surfaced to the caller. Standard tokio pattern; awareness only.
- **M3** — `tests/helpers/http2-echo-server/src/main.rs`'s deterministic-echo body alphabetic-sort relies on `BTreeMap` insertion-order semantics. This is correct (BTreeMap is sorted by key) but a future refactor to `HashMap` would silently break the differential body equivalence. Doc-comment at `:165-168` flags this. Awareness only.
- **M4** — `tests/differential/src/backend.rs::Http2EchoBackend` inherits 02.2 REVIEW M1's `*EchoBackend::Drop` polling loop blocking on `std::thread::sleep` from a tokio-runtime thread. **Standing inventory carryforward.**
- **M5** — Cargo.lock cadence carryforward (phase-04.1 REVIEW M5/M9). 05.3 introduced no new top-level workspace deps; the `tests/helpers/http2-echo-server/` member registration is the only Cargo.lock churn. **Standing inventory carryforward — phase 06+ may finally close this if a workspace-dep-introducing task forces a doctrine pick.**
- **M6** — phase-04.1 M-claim (`drive_http1` per-function unit test). 05.3 introduced no new H1 surfaces; M-claim continues unchanged.
- **M7** — phase-04.1 M7 (`TlsAcceptingHandler.inner` concrete-typed). 05.3 did not generalize the dispatch-handler type; M7 continues unchanged. The H2+TLS rejection (`Http2OverTlsNotSupported` parse-time gate landed in 05.2; symmetric runtime bail at `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs:269-277`) remains the explicit gate.
- **M8** — phase-04.1 M1/M2/M4 (header-diff value-comparison; body-drain idle silent Ok; strip_port IPv6-Host). Fixture 0010's H2-shaped responses don't surface duplicate headers, body stalls, or IPv6-Host conditions; H1-flavored carryforwards continue unchanged.

Small footprint (~30 LoC validator extension + ~10 LoC per-fixture-shape unit tests); closes the silent-misconfiguration window before phase 06+ touches access-log emission.

### Phase-06.1 rollovers (from REVIEW.md §3–§7)

The 06.1 REVIEW (HEAD `36fedd8`; reviewed range `1f7661a..a5f795c`; landed at `499e374`) concluded **Approved with M-track follow-ups** — zero Critical, **2 Important findings (I1 admin handler lacks idle read timeout — slow-loris-style resource hold; I2 `serialize_response` injects 5 standard headers unconditionally — duplicate-header hazard if a future endpoint sets the same name)**, 6 Minor findings M1-M6 (all dispositioned in REVIEW §4). One in-phase review-fix commit closed a substantive finding before propagation: `6b957ae` task 11 follow-up (restored 4 non-negotiable admin response headers `cache-control` / `x-content-type-options` / `server` / `date` after the phase-01 admin migration silently dropped them — exemplary D-3.5 / D-3.6 self-caught regression cadence).

Items closed in 06.1:

- **05.3 REVIEW I3** (placeholder `<CI run URL TBD by controller after push>` evidence-discipline regression): **CLOSED structurally** at the state-4 evidence anchor. PROGRESS Task 14 quotes a real CI run URL `https://github.com/pgdad/envoy-rust/actions/runs/25625271032` with HEAD SHA `36fedd8` and timestamp `2026-05-10T09:33:41Z`. The discipline regression flagged in 05.3 is corrected in 06.1 — every state-4 phase-done verification commit going forward should follow this evidence shape (CI URL + HEAD SHA + completion timestamp + per-gate quoted output).

Items carrying forward to 06.2 / 06.3 (recommended closure-targets):

- **I1 (Important; admin handler lacks idle read timeout — slow-loris-style resource hold).** `crates/envoy-admin/src/handler.rs:44-67` `read_request` loops on `stream.read(...).await` until either `MAX_REQUEST_HEAD = 8 KiB` is exceeded, `find_crlf_crlf` succeeds, or the peer closes (n == 0). A connected-but-silent client holds the connection open indefinitely, blocked on the kernel's read; the connection task in `JoinSet` stays parked. Compare the data-plane HCM at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:24` which sets `IDLE_READ_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5)` and wraps each per-request read in `tokio::time::timeout(IDLE_READ_TIMEOUT, ...)`. The shutdown path's `DRAIN_BUDGET = 5s` bounds the worst-case shutdown delay, but the steady-state hold is unbounded. **Fix sketch**: wrap the `stream.read(...)` call in `tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), ...)` and map the `Elapsed` to a 408 Request Timeout (or just close — both proxies' admin scrape clients should retry on close). Mechanical, scoped to one site, ~10 LoC. **Disposition: carry forward to 06.2 or 06.3.** Whichever sub-phase first touches the admin handler is the natural close site. The 06.2 SPEC has admin-side access logging OUT OF SCOPE per cross-sub-phase rule 6, so 06.2 likely does not touch the admin handler by default — but the 06.2 PLAN-writer may opportunistically close I1 with a small no-scope-creep PR if the LoC budget allows. The 06.3 SPEC's comprehensive stats-wiring scope may touch the admin handler indirectly; if so, I1 closes at the same task.

Items carrying forward to phase 08 (recommended closure-targets):

- **I2 (Important; `serialize_response` unconditionally injects 5 standard headers — duplicate-header hazard).** `crates/envoy-admin/src/handler.rs:96-120` walks `resp.headers` writing them out, then *unconditionally* writes `cache-control: no-cache, max-age=0`, `x-content-type-options: nosniff`, `server: envoy-rust`, `date: <RFC 7231>`, `connection: close`. The current 06.1 endpoints (`render_ready`, `render_stats`, `render_stats_prometheus`, `render_404`, `render_405`) only set `content-type` / `content-length` / `allow` (where applicable) — so today the assumption holds and there are no duplicates. But the abstraction is fragile: a future endpoint that sets `cache-control: max-age=300` (e.g., a `/server_info` endpoint that's safe to cache briefly) would emit *both* headers on the wire. **Fix sketch**: in `serialize_response`, dedupe by case-insensitive header name before writing the standard headers — write only the ones not already present in `resp.headers`. ~10 LoC. **The current behavior is not a bug** for the 06.1 endpoint set; it's a latent maintenance hazard that will surface in phase 08 when the full admin endpoint surface (config_dump, clusters, listeners, runtime, server_info, /quitquitquit) lands. **Disposition: carry forward to phase 08.**
- **M1 (admin handler `resp.reason.unwrap_or("OK")` wrong for non-200 statuses).** `handler.rs:98` uses `unwrap_or("OK")` — if a future endpoint constructs a `Response { status: 404, reason: None, ... }`, the wire output is `HTTP/1.1 404 OK\r\n` which is wrong. All current 06.1 endpoints set `reason: Some(...)`, so the path is unreachable today. **Fix sketch**: map `unwrap_or` to the standard reason for the status code, or change `Response.reason` to non-Optional `String` and require renderer to populate. **Disposition: carry forward to phase 08 alongside I2** — same site (`serialize_response`); whichever phase first lands a `reason: None` arm. May fold into 06.2/06.3 if the access-log emitter happens to need a reason-from-status-code helper.
- **M4 (`DRAIN_BUDGET` constant duplicated between `envoy-admin` and `envoy-listener`).** `crates/envoy-admin/src/handler.rs:22` defines `DRAIN_BUDGET: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5)` and `crates/envoy-listener/src/lib.rs:135` defines the identical const inline. Two sources of truth for the same drain budget. Today the values agree; future drift would be silent. **Fix sketch**: hoist to `envoy-listener` and re-export, or add a `// keep in lockstep` comment at one or both sites. **Disposition: carry forward to phase 08** (graceful-drain consolidation).

Items carrying forward indefinitely (awareness-only):

- **M2 (`find_crlf_crlf` is O(n²) under 8KiB cap).** Sub-millisecond worst-case (~64M byte comparisons under the 8KiB cap). Not a real-world hot path. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely.** Optional fix: track a `searched_up_to: usize` cursor with 3-byte CRLF-spanning overlap.
- **M3 (admin `SocketAddr` parse rejects hostnames).** `crates/envoy-admin/src/config.rs:21-35` builds `format!("{}:{}", sock.address, sock.port_value).parse::<SocketAddr>()` — hostname like `localhost` fails with `BadAddress`. Consistent with phase-04 + phase-05 listener bind sites at `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs:137-143`. Not a 06.1 regression. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — harmonize with whichever xDS-family phase first ships hostname-resolving listener bind.
- **M5 (in-process backstop SIGKILL-on-Drop posture).** `crates/envoy-bin/tests/admin_ready.rs:73-75`: `let _ = child.kill(); let _ = child.wait();`. Inherits the standing phase-02.2 REVIEW M1 chain (`*EchoBackend::Drop` polling-loop blocks on `std::thread::sleep`). **Disposition: inherits existing chain; no 06.1-specific action.**
- **M6 (empty `payload.bin` cross-fixture consistency tradeoff).** `tests/fixtures/0011-admin-stats-prometheus/inputs/payload.bin` is 0 bytes; harness drives the request flow synthetically via `Driver::AdminScrape`'s `pre_requests` field. Matches fixture 0010's shape (also 0 bytes empty payload.bin). Worth noting for stranger-readability — a stranger reading the fixture directory might assume `payload.bin` is meaningful. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — cross-fixture consistency vs stranger-readability tradeoff; both equally defensible.

- **Phase-05.3 REVIEW I1** (silent H1-listener × H2-cluster misnegotiation per ADR-0028 option-B deferral): **NOT engaged in 06.1** (admin is HTTP/1.1 only per cross-sub-phase rule 3; no H2 dispatch surface touched). Folded into **06.3 D14.3 as a Task-1 preamble** per parent-06 SPEC §3 + 06.3 SPEC §5. The 06.1 SPEC's `envoy-config` schema additions in D5 (`Admin.access_log_path` parse-and-ignore + `HCM.stat_prefix` parse-and-consume) do not regress the validator surface I1 will extend in 06.3.

- **Phase-04.1 REVIEW M5/M9** (Cargo.lock cadence ratification ADR): **CARRIES FORWARD UNCHANGED** — 06.1 introduced no new top-level Cargo deps under the recommended posture, so no cadence pick is forced. The Cargo.lock diff at `1f7661a..a5f795c` is +31 lines (workspace-member registrations for `envoy-admin` + `envoy-stats` only; no new external crates).

Recommendations forward-track (per REVIEW §7):

- **R-1** — The dot-tree contract (`http.<stat_prefix>.downstream_rq_total = value-exact`; `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total = name-required, value-may-differ`; `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_total = value-exact`) is in BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Stat-name mapping` rows 1-3 as authoritative. 06.3's comprehensive stats wiring extends — each new stat lands a row with the `value-exact` or `name-required, value-may-differ` disposition + rationale. **Do NOT silently widen the allow-list in fixture 0011's expectations.yaml** when new counters are wired; widen the table first, allow-list second.
- **R-2** — The Prometheus exposition emitter's name-vs-label shape divergence (2 envoy-rust-only allow-list entries paired with their bare-name upstream-Envoy counterparts) is documented in BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md "06.1 Prometheus exposition shape divergence" subsection. When a later phase introduces a `StatsTagExtractor`-equivalent that projects dynamic name segments back into Prometheus labels, the 2 envoy-rust-only entries and their paired upstream-Envoy counterparts on the envoy-only side **must drop together as a single commit**. Half-dropping introduces a real divergence.
- **R-3** — `Driver::AdminScrape` and `BodyRule::PrometheusExposition` are reusable. 06.3 extends fixture 0011's `expectations.yaml` to assert the comprehensive stat set; the harness shape needs no change.
- **R-4** — The `connection: close` discipline on the admin response is a phase-08 generalization point. 06.1's admin handler closes the connection after each response (no keep-alive). Phase 08 may want HTTP/1.1 keep-alive for repeated scrapes from a Prometheus poller.
- **R-5** — The I2 `serialize_response` 5-header dedupe and the I1 idle-read-timeout should land before phase 08 expands the admin endpoint surface. 06.2 or 06.3 SPEC §6 should signpost both. Mechanical, ~20 LoC total.
- **R-6** — `Admin.access_log_path` is parsed-and-ignored at 06.1; admin-side access logging is OUT OF SCOPE in 06.2 too. The field stays parse-and-ignored indefinitely from 06.1; if 06.3 or a later phase wires admin-side access logging, the field already exists in the schema and `AdminConfig` already carries it through — no migration needed.
- **R-7** — The HCM stat-prefix-namespacing pattern (`format!("http.{stat_prefix}.<name>", ...)`) is the convention. 06.3 extends with `..._2xx`, `..._3xx`, `..._4xx`, `..._5xx`, and (per Rule 4) `..._access_logs_total`. Use the same `format!` pattern; register at `HCMStats::register` time; expose via `HCMStats` field accessors. The `register` method is idempotent for same-kind so re-registration of the same stat under the same `stat_prefix` is safe.
- **R-8** — The `Counter::add(n)` API exists but is unused by 06.1's three counters. When 06.3 wires bulk-increment paths (e.g., `bytes_received`/`bytes_sent` counters that increment per-N-bytes rather than per-event), `add(n)` is the right primitive; `inc()` would be wrong. The API is in place; usage starts in 06.3 if the bytes-side counters land then.
- **R-9** — Pre-state-4 fmt discipline. The 06.1 Task 14 fmt-drift catch (13 files of accumulated drift across Tasks 2-13) is the precedent: per-task PROGRESS sections logged "clippy clean" but not "fmt clean," and the drift accumulated quietly. 06.2 / 06.3 / future phases should consider running `cargo fmt --all -- --check` at every PROGRESS-task close, not just at state-4. Or land a pre-commit hook (out of scope for in-phase work; signposts for a future infra-only phase if anyone scopes it).

Items closed in 06.2:

All carryforwards from 06.1 / 05.3 / 05.2 / 04.x / 02.2 carry through 06.2 unchanged.

Items carrying forward to 06.3 (recommended closure-targets):

- **I1 (Important; H1 dispatch-site state vars use `mut x = 0/default` rather than H2's `let x;` posture).** `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:313-316` initializes `let mut response_status_for_log: u16 = 0; let mut response_body_len: u64 = 0; let mut upstream_host_for_log: Option<String> = None; let mut response_headers_for_log: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new();` — the default-initialization defeats Rust's flow analysis. The H2 posture at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:134-137` is strictly stronger (`let response_status_for_log: u16; let response_body_len: u64; let response_headers_for_log: Vec<(String, String)>; let mut upstream_host_for_log_h2: Option<String> = None;`) — every match arm must write the uninitialized locals before they're read. PROGRESS Task 7 deviation 4 acknowledges the asymmetry (*"Minor style asymmetry vs the H1 path where all 4 are `mut`; documented here so reviewers don't flag it."*) but the tightening pass was deferred. **Fix sketch**: drop the `mut` and the `= 0` / `= None` / `= Vec::new()` defaults on the H1 site; verify all 5 H1 writer arms (synth + 4 proxy: no-endpoint-503, connect-fail-502, send-fail-502, success) still type-check. ~10 LoC at one site. **Disposition: carry forward to 06.3** or whichever phase next touches `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:319-457`'s match block.
- **I2 (Important; fixture-vs-contract User-Agent disposition divergence + empirically-wrong PROGRESS rationale).** Fixture 0012's `tests/fixtures/0012-access-log-file-sink/expectations.yaml` uses `rule: wildcard` for the `%REQ(USER-AGENT)%` token, but `docs/envoy-rust/BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md:138` declares the canonical disposition as `value-exact`. PROGRESS Task 10 deviation 2 explains the divergence as: "*`drive_http1` (the 04.1 helper) sets `User-Agent` to a default*". But `tests/differential/src/lib.rs:832-855` shows `drive_http1` does NOT inject any `User-Agent` header. Either (a) the wildcard is hiding a real envoy-vs-envoy-rust divergence (e.g., upstream Envoy emits some default User-Agent that envoy-rust doesn't, or vice versa), or (b) `wildcard` is unnecessarily loose and the rule should tighten to `exact: "-"`. **Fix sketch**: 06.3 should empirically diagnose by running the fixture with `exact: "-"` and observing the failure mode, OR add a logging tap to capture what each proxy sees for User-Agent. If (a), update BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md row 12 with the actual divergence rationale; if (b), tighten fixture 0012's expectations.yaml to `exact: "-"`. ~15 LoC of empirical work + doc edit. **Disposition: carry forward to 06.3.**
- **M3 (Minor; fixture 0012's README.md documents pre-CI-fix paths).** `tests/fixtures/0012-access-log-file-sink/README.md` lists `/tmp/0012-envoy-access.log` and `/tmp/0012-envoy-rust-access.log`, but the actual `envoy.yaml`, `envoy-rust.yaml`, and `expectations.yaml` use `/tmp/0012-envoy-mount/access.log` and `/tmp/0012-envoy-rust-mount/access.log` (the parent-directory-bind-mount form from CI-fix commit `4aba10b`). **Fix sketch**: edit README.md to match the post-CI-fix paths; add a one-line note explaining the parent-dir mount strategy and cross-reference Task 10's CI-fix in PROGRESS. ~5 LoC of doc edit. **Disposition: carry forward to 06.3** or fold into any 06.3-task that touches the fixture.

Items carrying forward indefinitely (awareness-only):

- **M1 (Minor; `Http1Error::AccessLogOpen` wraps as `message: String`, losing source-chain typing).** `crates/envoy-http1/src/error.rs` declares `AccessLogOpen { message: String }` with `#[error("failed to open access log sink: {message}")]`. PROGRESS Task 6 dev 2 explains: thiserror treats a field named `source` as a nested `std::error::Error` and `String` doesn't implement `Error`. Same shape as 06.1's `StatsRegistration { stat_prefix, message: String }`. **Fix sketch**: use a non-`source` field with explicit `#[source]` attribute (e.g., `#[source] inner: AccessLogError`). **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — whichever phase next refactors typed-error chains across crates can tighten both sites together.
- **M2 (Minor; `BodyRule::ByteExact` doesn't assert against a literal expected body).** `tests/fixtures/0012-access-log-file-sink/expectations.yaml` declares `expected_body: { kind: byte_exact }`. Per `tests/differential/src/lib.rs::assert_body_rule`, `BodyRule::ByteExact` only enforces byte-equality between upstream Envoy and envoy-rust — it does NOT assert either body actually equals `"ok\n"`. The 04.1 / 04.3 / 05.2 / 05.3 fixtures all use the same shape, so this is pre-existing — but the access-log fixture's `BYTES_SENT` token IS asserted as `exact: "3"` which catches that regression via the token rule. **Fix sketch**: introduce `BodyRule::ByteExactValue { value: String }` (mirrors `Http1BodyRule::ByteExact { body: String }` that already exists for the legacy 04.x driver). **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — phase-wide pre-existing pattern, not 06.2-specific.
- **M4 (Minor; `/tmp/0012-envoy-mount/` is a process-shared host path; parallel test jobs could collide).** `tests/differential/src/lib.rs:1388-1431` extracts the parent dir from the fixture's `expected_access_log_paths` and creates+chmods it host-side under `/tmp/0012-envoy-mount`. Today the testcontainers harness implicitly serializes Docker-gated tests (each fixture acquires the container's exposed port and tears down at drop), so the collision is mostly hypothetical — but the path being hardcoded means a future parallelism push (e.g., `cargo nextest`'s test sharding) would surface this. **Fix sketch**: prefix the access-log directory with a process-PID or per-fixture tempdir (`/tmp/0012-envoy-mount-{pid}/`), template-substitute into the YAML at fixture-render time. ~15 LoC. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — activates only under future parallelism push (likely a CI-infra phase post-08).
- **M5 (Minor; H2 access-log test settles via 200ms `tokio::time::sleep` rather than awaiting connection task).** `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs` test fixture sleeps for 200ms because "*the H2 conn future does not return until the server side EOFs the TCP stream*" (PROGRESS Task 7 deviation 3). Acceptable for a unit test today but adds CI runtime + potential flakiness on a loaded runner. **Fix sketch**: use `h2::server::SendResponse::stream_id()` / a `Notify` shared between the test and the server task to deterministically signal emission completion. ~30 LoC. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — pattern-equivalent to the 06.1 backstop's `thread::sleep(100ms)` posture (M5 from 06.1 REVIEW chain) and inherits the standing 02.2 REVIEW M1 chain.

Recommendations forward-track (per REVIEW §7):

- **R-1** — The `access_logs_total` counter (parent-06 SPEC §3 D15.3) lands in 06.3 at the HCM dispatch site. 06.2 intentionally added NO stats coupling; the HCM dispatch site at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:484-488` and the symmetric H2 `finalize_h2_stream` site are the clean seam for 06.3's additive counter increment. Per parent §6 Rule 4, the counter increments at queue-enter time (BEFORE the await), so emission failures do NOT deflate the count. The increment lives in the `if !config.access_log.is_empty()` block: increment once per sink per record, before `sink.emit(&record).await`. The 06.3 SPEC §3 D15.3 should signpost this exact site to avoid double-incrementing.
- **R-2** — BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Access log field mapping` row 12 (`%REQ(USER-AGENT)%`) should be reconciled with fixture 0012's `wildcard` rule (couples with I2 above).
- **R-3** — The H1 vs H2 access-log state-init asymmetry (I1) should land in 06.3 (~10 LoC at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:313-316`).
- **R-4** — Format-string customization remains OUT of scope through parent-06 close. Per parent-06 SPEC §4 and 06.2 SPEC §4, `%FILTER_STATE%`, `%DYNAMIC_METADATA%`, `%TRAILER(...)%`, custom `%START_TIME(format-string)%`, `%REQ_WITHOUT_QUERY(...)%`, json-format, typed_json_format, and access-log filtering all defer to later observability-family phases. 06.3's SPEC §4 should re-state this explicitly so the planner doesn't get tempted; the existing `FileAccessLog` schema at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs` (with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`) rejects any attempt to add these fields without an explicit schema extension.
- **R-5** — The Task 6 follow-up pattern (review-fix landing as a small dedicated commit between Task 6 and Task 7) is a precedent worth preserving. `f73face` lands a single-issue fix (gate `from_file_for_test` behind `#[cfg(feature = "test-util")]`) as a named "Task 6 follow-up" commit with its own PROGRESS section. This contrasts with the 06.1 Task 11 follow-up commit (`6b957ae`) that restored admin headers. Both demonstrate that mid-execution review-fixups land cleanly as small named commits, not amendments to the original task — D-3.5 / D-3.6 in action.
- **R-6** — The bind-mount-parent-directory CI-fix shape (`tests/differential/src/lib.rs:1374-1432`) should be inherited by any future writable-mount fixture. PROGRESS Task 11 "Carry-to-REVIEW.md notes" flags this: *"Linux Docker bind-mount file-level mode bits don't reliably propagate write permissions; mount a parent directory at 0o777 and let the in-container process create the file fresh."* 06.3 D17.3's fixture-0011-comprehensive-stats extension doesn't add new writable mounts (stats endpoints are read-only), so 06.3 itself doesn't need the pattern — but the future phase that first adds a writable mount (e.g., access-log + gRPC ALS sink fixtures, RTDS file-source phases) should consult the in-tree comment first.
- **R-7** — A pre-push parity gate (running CI Docker locally on Linux, or `act` on the workflow) would have caught the cross-Docker bind-mount issue at state-3 rather than state-4. PROGRESS Task 11 "Carry-to-REVIEW.md notes" raises this. Out of 06.3 scope (it's a CI-infra concern), but worth signposting in a future infra-only phase.
- **R-8** — The `BodyRule::ByteExact` extension (M2) should land alongside whichever phase next surfaces a fixture-specific body assertion need.
- **R-9** — The `Http1Error::AccessLogOpen { message: String }` vs `StatsRegistration { stat_prefix, message: String }` pattern should be reconciled in whichever phase next refactors typed-error chains.
- **R-10** — The `upstream_service_time` extraction reads from the captured `response_headers_for_log`, NOT from the live response. `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs` explicitly pushes the `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` header into `response_headers_for_log` so the dispatch site's `extract_upstream_service_time` helper can observe it (since the live downstream wire output is already consumed). 06.3 should re-verify this when the router-proxy fixture (whichever first exercises the upstream-service-time access-log field) lands; today fixture 0012's direct_response path emits `-` for this token on both sides and the integration is structurally exercised but not asserted.

### Phase-05 ADR ledger (for reference)

Landed ADRs:

- **ADR-0022** (split phase 05 into sub-phases 05.1, 05.2, 05.3 by surface boundary) — landed at parent-05 state-2 commit `f1804a7`.
- **ADR-0023** (`ClusterType::StrictDns` accepted; `LOGICAL_DNS` deferred) — landed at 05.1 Task 1 commit `bfabcb6` (inline at Task 1 per the ADR-0021 inline-at-Task-1 precedent). Closes phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 (positive `Static` regression guard, enabled by introducing the second `ClusterType` variant). **Partially closes phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1** (cross-phase Docker-gated regression — the original Envoy v1.33 `malformed IP address: host.docker.internal` startup error is no longer triggered; fixture 0008's residual upstream-routing defect closed substantively in 05.4).
- **ADR-0024** (`Cluster.dns_lookup_family` field + `DnsLookupFamily` enum in envoy-config; parse-only with runtime non-consumption deliberate) — landed at 05.4 Task 1 commit `3d0af4a` (inline at Task 1 per the ADR-0021 / ADR-0023 inline-at-Task-1 precedent). The 3-variant enum (V4Only / V6Only / Auto) is the minimum viable coverage of Envoy v1.33's surface; runtime non-consumption is doctrine-correct per D-3.6 minimalism (the runtime-extension trigger is named: "whichever later phase first needs envoy-rust to filter resolved addresses by family lands the runtime extension then, with its own test"). Substantively closes phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's IPv6/IPv4 selection regression at the upstream Envoy boundary (when paired with Task 2's 5-fixture YAML edit setting `dns_lookup_family: V4_ONLY` on the `host.docker.internal` clusters).
- **ADR-0026** (`Listener.listener_filters: Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` parse-and-ignore field in envoy-config — NEW PATTERN) — landed at 05.4 Task 3 commit `f1dbe12`. Enables envoy.yaml fixtures to declare listener filters that envoy-rust does NOT execute (envoy-rust performs SNI dispatch at the rustls layer; phase 03.2 architectural choice). The `Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` opaque-storage type preserves the ability to inspect without typing. Enables fixture 0006's explicit `tls_inspector` block needed on macOS Docker. **Bounds future extension explicitly**: "Whichever later phase first needs to ACTUALLY EXECUTE a listener filter lands a typed-variant extension on the field plus a runtime dispatch arm — not a new ADR (extending an existing pattern)."
- **ADR-0025** (suppress synthetic `content-length: 0` on empty-body requests in `envoy-http1::Client` per RFC 7230 §3.3.2 + Envoy v1.33 parity) — landed at 05.4 Task 5 commit `01edie3`. Pass-through unchanged for explicit Content-Length AND for non-empty bodies; suppression triggers only when the body is `None` or empty `&[]`. Closes phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's fixture-0008 byte-equal-echo regression (upstream Envoy was returning the synthetic `content-length: 0` line in the deterministic-echo body, which the differential gate rejected as a byte mismatch). 05.3's H2 codec emission decision should reference this ADR + the `body_is_nonempty` predicate per REVIEW §4 R-4.
- **ADR-0027** (`http` crate (`http::Request` / `http::Response` / `http::HeaderMap`) typed-surface scoping — `http` graduates from transitive-only via `h2` to a direct workspace foundation, scoped narrowly to `envoy-http2`'s codec-edge translations) — landed at 05.2 Task 1 commit `9c2b2fd` (inline at Task 1 alongside the `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml` scaffold creation, per the ADR-0021 / ADR-0023 / ADR-0024 inline-at-Task-1 precedent). Mirrors ADR-0021's `regex` scoping discipline. Renumbering provenance: projected as conditional ADR-0024 in 05.2 SPEC §7 + parent-05 SPEC §7; renumbered to ADR-0027 at landing time because phase 05.4 landed ADR-0024 / ADR-0025 / ADR-0026 between SPEC writeup and 05.2 execution. Mirrors the phase-03 ADR-renumbering precedent.
- **ADR-0028** (resolution of the `envoy-http1` ↔ `envoy-http2` cycle introduced by 05.3 SPEC §3 D4 router dispatch) — landed at 05.3 Task 6 commit `366af8b`. **Documented D-3.5 in-execution deviation from 05.3 SPEC §7's "no new ADRs projected" projection.** The cycle was discovered when the controller attempted to add `envoy-http2` as a `[dependencies]` entry on `crates/envoy-http1/Cargo.toml` to wire the H1 router-arm dispatch — `envoy-http2` already depends on `envoy-http1` for the listener-side `HCMConfig` type alias landed in 05.2, so adding the reverse edge would create a Cargo cycle. The ADR adopts option (B) — deferral: the H1 router-arm at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs`'s `BuildOutcome::Proxy` does not dispatch to H2 clusters in 05.3; the cluster's `upstream_protocol` field is queried but the H1 path returns a 502 with a doctrine-line body when the cluster is `Http2`-flagged. The H2 router-arm dispatch lives at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs` and dispatches symmetrically to either H1 or H2 clusters via the new `envoy-http2::Client`. Future-extension trigger: option (A) — extract a third crate `envoy-http-router` carrying the protocol-agnostic router-walk + `BuildOutcome` + dispatch — is the natural close site if a future phase needs cross-protocol cluster sharing on the H1 listener. **Bounds future extension explicitly**: "Whichever later phase first needs the H1-listener × H2-cluster path lands the `envoy-http-router` extraction; this ADR's option-(B) deferral is then superseded by reference."

The phase-05 ADRs landed in DECISIONS.md in landing-time order, NOT numeric order: ADR-0023 (05.1 Task 1) → ADR-0024 (05.4 Task 1) → ADR-0026 (05.4 Task 3) → ADR-0025 (05.4 Task 5) → ADR-0027 (05.2 Task 1) → ADR-0028 (05.3 Task 6). The append-only ledger discipline is preserved (no retroactive renumbering). The DECISIONS.md ledger head reached **ADR-0028** at the parent-05 close commit `82c26b8`; subsequently advanced to **ADR-0029** at the parent-06 state-1+state-2 combined-recovery commit `1f7661a` (parent-06 split decision).

Phase 05 final ADR ledger summary (parent-05 closed at commit `82c26b8`):

- **Projected at parent-05 state-2 SPEC**: ADR-0022 (split decision); conditional ADR-0024 (`h2` foundation grant — landed as ADR-0027), conditional ADR-0025 (h2spec gate-mechanics — explicitly NOT landed per 05.2 PROGRESS Task 13 rationale).
- **Actually landed**: ADR-0022 / 0023 / 0024 / 0025 / 0026 / 0027 / 0028 (7 ADRs across 4 sub-phases). One unforeseen ADR (ADR-0028 cycle resolution at 05.3 Task 6) — well within D-3.5's permission for in-execution deviation. Phase 05's ADR density is the highest yet (7 ADRs vs phase-04's 2, phase-03's 3, phase-02's 4) reflecting the multi-foundation H2 scope (h2 / http / DnsLookupFamily / listener_filters / content-length suppression / cycle resolution).

The other two variants (`RouterNotTerminal` / `DuplicateRouter`) remain defense-in-depth-only by design — the `envoy-config` validator is the operator-facing catch. This partial closure matches the original 07.1 REVIEW M2 disposition exactly; no further action.

Items carrying forward (the 3 Minor findings — all awareness-only / close-opportunistically; **none has a hard-gated owner phase; none gated the 07.2 state-6 close-out**):

- **M1 (Minor; severed `position` plumbing in `HttpFilterInstance::build` → `map_entry`).** `FilterPipeline::build_from_config` threads the filter-chain `position` via `.enumerate()` into `HttpFilterInstance::build`, but `build` (`crates/envoy-filter/src/instance.rs`) ignores it (`_position: usize`), and `map_entry` (`crates/envoy-filter/src/header_mutation.rs`) hardcodes `position: 0` in the defense-in-depth `FilterError::UnsupportedFilterType` it constructs. The plumbing exists end-to-end except the last two hops. **Not a correctness bug** — the `envoy-config` validator carries the real entry index in `UnsupportedHeaderMutationAppendAction`; `map_entry`'s construction is defense-in-depth that only fires if a caller bypasses the validator. **Fix sketch (preferred):** drop the dead `_position` parameter from `build` (option b — consistent with the 07.1 REVIEW I1 "delete dead plumbing" posture). **Disposition: close opportunistically** — fold into whichever future HTTP-filter-family phase next adds a non-HeaderMutation `HttpFilterInstance` variant (which re-opens `instance.rs::build`). No hard-gated owner.
- **M2 (Minor; `apply_mutations` Overwrite is O(n²) in the unlikely many-entries case).** Each `RuntimeAppendAction::Overwrite` runs a full `headers.retain(...)` with a per-element `to_ascii_lowercase()` allocation. For 07.2's 2-4-entry fixtures this is correct and trivially cheap. 07.2 SPEC §6 signpost 3 explicitly defers the optimization per YAGNI. **Fix sketch (if ever needed):** `eq_ignore_ascii_case` without allocating, or a removal set built once per call, alongside the `Arc<Vec<...>>` refactor. **Disposition: carry forward indefinitely** — acknowledged-and-accepted YAGNI; no named owner; activates only if a future deployment surfaces large mutation lists.
- **M3 (Minor; fixture 0013's `expected_body` byte-exact assertion coupled to upstream Envoy's request-header injection set).** `tests/fixtures/0013-http-filter-header-mutation/expectations.yaml` hardcodes the exact echoed header set; this relies on `envoy.yaml`'s `request_headers_to_remove` block enumerating every header Envoy v1.33 injects (the same posture as fixture 0008). Correct today (CI run `25887571566` green) but a future Envoy version-pin bump that adds a new default request header would silently break the body-exact assertion. The coupling is documented in the fixture `README.md` but not at the assertion site. **Fix sketch:** add a cross-reference comment in `expectations.yaml`. **Disposition: close opportunistically** — natural forced-close site is the future `ENVOY_TARGET.md` version-bump phase (its own phase per D-3.7) that re-baselines the differential suite.

Matches project precedent (one decision per ADR; ADR-0014 / 0016 / 0021 / 0023 each land one decision).
